# aterm-release: spec recovery from its 885 unit tests

> Status 2026-10-16, after the merge with the tests-and-gates audit: the rows naming 168 tests
> it deleted and 3 specs whose functions it deleted are gone, and the spec rows no longer cite
> those tests. Counts elsewhere in this file were taken before.

> Status 2026-10-17: the rows naming 6 more deleted tests and 6 more specs whose functions are
> gone (the deleted `build.rs`, the fixture-guard and census copies of step 4, the config-table
> rustflags readers) are gone too. The §1 kind, gap and tier tables count the 362 specs left.
> Other counts in this file were taken before.

> Status 2026-10-17 (the D27 candidate gate deleted: publishing a Trust seal is no longer gated
> on a proof-count comparison): `ship trust candidate`, `ship trust compare`, `trust_candidate.rs`,
> `trust_compare.rs`, the retraction ledger and `tests/it/{candidate_replay,d27_refute}.rs` are
> gone, and with them their 20 specs (the candidate's, the comparison's, the ledger's, the
> obligation keys' and the record parser's) and the rows of the 66 tests that sampled only them.
> `trust_oblig` keeps the record `ship trust run --obligations` writes, and its specs
> `anchor-of-char-span`, `sources-resolve-file-key` and `reduce-report-accepts-only-whole-v2`. The
> §1 tables count the 342 specs left.

> Status 2026-10-17 (the lane's memory guard deleted: only the candidate gate built one, and
> `ship trust run --guard` was its leftover): `Guard`, `trust_lane::guard` and the `Checks::guard`
> hook are gone, and with them the spec `guard-verdict-and-readers`, its test
> `the_guard_says_which_bound_was_crossed`, and `the_memory_guard_ends_a_check_and_says_why` with
> `run-slots-scheduler`'s guard clause. The §1 tables count the 341 specs left.

> Status 2026-10-22 (init-system S15: no `gh` in the release): every GitHub read and write is REST
> through curl (`src/github.rs` over `aterm_update_core::github_rest`). The spec
> `curl-help-preflight` and its test are gone with the curl `--help` preflight (curl's own
> refusal of its argv is exit 2, `NeverSent`), `github-auth-host-pinned` became
> `rel-github-rest-lane` with the loopback tests of `src/github_tests.rs`, `gh_verdict` left
> `rel-provision-verdicts`, and the name-based download refusal left with `gh_raw` (an asset is
> read by its id alone). The §1 tables count the 340 specs left. On review, the post-cut reads and
> the site sync's REST curls start through `StepChildren` (`github::Launched`), and
> `rel-one-shot-curl-args` and `rs-release-object-identity` name the functions that replaced
> `OneShotPost::curl_args` and `release_identity_jq`.

> Status 2026-10-25 (TRUST-MEM, docs/DESIGN-adaptive-build-2026-10-24.md): the Trust lane's
> memory comes from the machine, not from total RAM. `auto-and-heavy-slots` is `auto-slots`
> (cores only), `heavy_slots` and the heavy flag are gone, and `run-slots-scheduler` admits
> each check's memory claim (`claim-need`) at its launch gate instead; its heavy test is
> replaced by the claim tests. `memory-row-selection-and-guard` applies the RAM / 8 model only
> under a prover whose `trustc -Z help` lists the ceiling flag, and the new `peak-refusal`
> refuses, under any prover, a library whose recorded peak with its margin (its claim) is not
> below the RAM. `read_costs` takes each library's newer row of the seed and the history. The
> §1 tables count the 349 specs there are (the kind table had missed `fx-land-unrecorded`,
> `fx-release-answers` and `listing-whole-or-timed-out`).

> Status 2026-10-16: this is the crate's migration worklist. Each spec below is proved through
> Trust/Clean about the compiled Rust function; then the tests it subsumes are deleted. Nothing has
> been proved or deleted yet. The non-spec tests in §4 carry their own actions.
>
> **Blocked: `exact-delete-absence`** (2026-10-16), and with it every spec in this crate, by gap 9 at
> crate scope. On Trust seal `150efe4a` the evidence-grade `targo trust check -p aterm-release`
> refuses the whole unit before verifying anything: `error: verified Targo refuses unit aterm-release
> … in-process proc-macro dependencies [aterm-serde-derive …::aterm_serde_derive] share rustc's argv,
> memory, stderr, and artifact filesystem and can forge compiler-message/TRUSTJSON proof transport or
> mutate authenticated outputs. Evidence-grade verification currently enforces a no-proc-macro TCB
> boundary`; report.json then has no row for any fn (`transport:missing-json`, COULD NOT RUN). The
> contract itself is ready: with `trust_contract_fn!`/`verify_island!` local to `publish.rs`
> (`tests/it` mounts that file without `main.rs`) and the arms taking `$vis:vis const fn`, the clause
> `ensures result == (preexisting_absence_is_converged || delete_attempted) by
> exact_delete_absence_is_disjunction` with the Clean theorem `∀ (a b : Bool),
> trust_import_aterm_release__publish__exact_delete_absence_is_converged a b = (a || b)` (proof:
> `simp only [<the import>]`) is accepted on the advisory lane (`--allow-l0-gaps`): the fn's row is 3/3
> proved, postcondition `clean-kernel-citation`, `Certified`, KernelRechecked. That lane forfeits the
> evidence-grade TCB boundary, so it does not count; the code is unchanged and no test is deleted.
> Note the import name carries the module path (`__publish__`); the bare
> `trust_import_aterm_release__<fn>` is rejected as `AutoBoundHeadOverApplied`. Lifting it needs the
> serde derives expanded out of this crate's dependency graph, or a seal that admits proc macros.

Source: `crates/aterm-release` (read only). Every `#[test]` in `build.rs`, `src/**` and `tests/**`
was read together with the code it reaches, in three chunks (347 + 348 + 185 = 880). The 880 test
names were then matched mechanically against the files: every spec cites only tests that exist, and
every test is either cited by a spec or listed in §4. Nothing was compiled or run. The claims come
from reading the code.

Result: **371 specs**, covering **849 tests**. **30 tests are not spec samples** (§4):
7 delete, 21 move-to-bench, 2 becomes-spec-after-fix.

The chunks overlapped on nine properties seen from different files; each was merged into one spec
(`rel-expected-embedded-pin`, `rel-channel-asset-set`, `rel-changelog-roll`, `rel-devid-preflight`,
`rs-scan-release-page`, `journal-roundtrip-valid`, `remeasure-fold-own-key`, `judge-floor-rule`,
`rs-cli-parse-grammar`). Spec ids keep their chunk prefix (`rel-` for the library modules read in
chunk 1, unprefixed for the Trust-lane and integration modules of chunk 2, `fx-/rf-/rl-/rs-/sc-/tg-/ss-/sn-/tf-`
for the integration files of chunk 3) so a reader can trace each back.

## 0. Notation used in the statements

Paths. Test references are `<path>::<test fn>` with `<path>` relative to `crates/aterm-release`
(`build.rs`, `src/…`, `tests/it/…`). Function references are
`<module or file>::<item>` in this crate unless prefixed by another crate (`aterm_update_core::…`,
`aterm_spec::…`, `atpkg_keys::…`).

Strings and digests.
- `lines(s)` = `str::lines(s)`. `trim` is `str::trim`. `chars(s)` counts Unicode scalar values.
- `GL(s)` = `github_length(s)` = Σ `char::len_utf16` over s (UTF-16 code units). `L` =
  `GITHUB_RELEASE_BODY_LIMIT` = 125 000.
- `hex40(s)`: 40 bytes in `[0-9a-f]`. `hex64(s)` = `canonical_sha256(s)`: 64 bytes in `[0-9a-f]`.
  `HEX40U(s)`: 40 bytes in `[0-9A-F]`.
- "Err naming X" / "Err 'X'" means the error text contains X. Wording beyond the named substrings is
  prose, not part of any spec; asserting it is prose-pinning and is dropped.
- `canonical X.Y.Z`: three decimal u64 components, no leading zeros, no prefix or suffix.
  `version_triple(v)` = `Some((a,b,c))` iff v is "a.b.c" with decimal u64 parts.

Effects and traces.
- `Calls(r)`: the ordered argv vectors (program, then args) an injected runner closure, fake git,
  fake world or fake rustup received during one call. `Env(c)`, `Dir(c)`: the env overrides
  (set / removed) and `current_dir` of a built `Command`. A "Command builder" spec is about
  (program, args, Env, Dir) and nothing else.
- `Fs`, `Git`: file-system and git object/ref state before a call; `Fs'`, `Git'` after. An
  *effect* spec is a contract over `(Fs, Git) → (result, Fs', Git')`; it waits on gap 8. Where a pure
  core can be split out, the statement names it, and that core can be proved first.
- A *trace* (`apple-hooks-trace`, `notarize-and-package-order`) is the ordered list of trait-method
  calls made on an injected `AppleTools`/`Packager`; the spec quantifies over every implementation and
  every result of each call.

Release flow (`release.rs::run_with`). A world W = (Backend state, release dir R =
`home/aterm-release` holding `JOURNAL_FILE`, `RUNNING_FILE`, `RUNNING_CHILD_FILE*` notes).
`run(W,o)` gives `(W', Outcome{code,last}, Out = the B.say lines, Calls = B's methods invoked)`.
The mutating set is M = {sync, bump, move_to, commit_gates(dry=false), stage, reserve, build,
stage_fixtures, verify, publish, source_publication, cut, after_cut, land_fixtures, ask, resolve,
withdraw, reexec}; `Calls|M` is `Calls` restricted to M. Steps are `Step::ALL` = [1 checks,
2 sync, 3 commit, 4 stage, 5 build, 6 verify, 7 ready, 8 publish, 9 cut, 10 after-cut, 11 check];
`step.at()` = "n/11 name".
`Final` = `last` ∈ RELEASED | PREPARED | DRY RUN | REFUSED at s: why — public. remedy |
INTERRUPTED at s (by SIGx) — public. remedy. Code: 0 for RELEASED, PREPARED and a would-go-ahead DRY
RUN; 1 for a decisive REFUSED; 3 for REFUSED: COULD NOT RUN; 130 for INTERRUPTED. `Tail` = origin's
ledger tail as `B.origin_tail` reports it.

Cut journal (`publish::Journal`). `valid(j)`: `j.done` is a duplicate-free prefix of
`STEPS` = [lock, sign, selfcheck, tag, publish, unlock]; `j.version` is canonical; `j.commit` is
40 hex; `min_build ≤ build_number`; `signature_required ⇒ signature_pubkey` is Some;
`signature_required ∧ sign ∈ done ⇒ manifest_signed`; `manifest_signed ⇒ signature_required`;
`format == JOURNAL_FORMAT`. `finished(h)` ⇔ "unlock" ∈ h.done.

Lease (`publish.rs`). The remote holds at most `RELEASE_LEASE_REF` (owner oid) and
`PUBLISHER_FENCE_REF` (owner, token). `LeaseRelease` ∈ {Released, AlreadyAbsent, AlreadySuperseded}.

Trust lane (`trust_oblig`, `trust_facts`, `trust_lane`). `Measured` is read from one check's log
by `Measured::of_log`.

Site (`site.rs`). `sync(opts, env, World, out)` → `Result<Outcome, Failure>`; World is the injected
github/download/live/build_module/optimise/deploy/launch (deploy = Firebase Hosting's REST API, `site::hosting`). `Owned(site)` = public/{index,aterm,terminal,
releases}.html, assets/releases.css, install.sh, sitemap.xml, assets/term/{engine.js, ENGINE_SOURCE,
aterm_wasm.*}. `Owed` is the marker `.git/OWED_MARK`.

Snapshot (`snapshot.rs`). `Tree(repo, rev)` is rev's recursive tree read from objects, each submodule
at its gitlink. A *take* is the materialized directory under the held cache.

Grid (`publish.rs`). `VALUE_COL` = 13, `LABEL_MAX` = 10; a token is a maximal run of non-space chars.

Refinement specs bind production transitions to the `ty_model!` models in `aterm-spec`
(ReleaseChannelFloor, ReleaseClaimLanding, ReleaseDurablePostIntent, ReleaseHistoricalRecovery,
ReleasePublisherFence, the derived channel-head model, `release_journal_prefix_model`,
`trust_facts_reuse_model`): every observed state, projected onto model variables, must be reachable by
an enabled healthy action, and each kept mutant must not be.

Gap numbers are the rows of §7 of `docs/AUDIT-trust-for-test-migration-2026-10-15.md`: 0 seal,
1 non-scalar carriers, 2 `&self`/`&mut self` views, 3 E6 body widening, 4 callee-ensures composition,
5 quantified clauses, 6 plain-Rust clause publication, 7 loops, 8 effects, 9 proc-macro (serde/toml),
10 model→code refinement, 11 generics, 12 cross-crate citation, 13 closures/dyn/higher-order,
14 Clean elaborator. Every spec is blocked by gap 0 (the seal); the column lists the gaps beyond it,
and `0` means the seal is the only blocker.

handle)`, `blocking_gaps (audit §7; gap 0 implied)`.

What the floor's run also had earns nothing at any load. --update-ratchet sets or raises no floor from a starved run whose credit > 0, nor from one a backstop stopped that kept no record; a floor it writes rewrites c's BACKSTOPS rows to a head row with the run's cancelled count and then its backstopped set, written with the ratchet (each staged beside itself, then moved, so a failed write leaves both as they were). --update-ratchet never lowers a same-prover floor; lowers another prover's only with a column-5 reason; records first floors under the measuring prover; raises to the high-water mark; rewrites a corrected total with the floor unchanged. Lives after the 16th. A rival tail reaching n refuses with NOTHING burned (no line, no release commit on origin, checkout clean on the published commit) and names the re-run, which reserves a strictly higher n. A one-shot POST is sent once and read by its exit (`--fail-with-body`: a 4xx is exit 22, `Refused`). A listing is read through `Link` pagination to its end; a page that advertises more and holds nothing is refused on every attempt. An asset is read by its id: the asset API's redirect is followed only onto the CDN origins (anything else is refused and never asked), anonymously. Reads and idempotent mutations are made at most 3 times; a definitive answer (a 4xx but 408/429, a refused redirect, a local refusal) once; none once the release has caught an interrupt. The post-cut reads (`release_steps::Real::github_read`) and the site sync's (`site::Real::github`) start each curl through their launch (`github::Launched` of `StepChildren`: a session of its own, the interrupt handed to it, noted while it runs, none once interrupted), so a SIGTERM sent to the release alone ends the curl and nothing more is asked; a launch's refusal to start one is a local refusal, never asked again.	src/github_tests.rs::a_draft_is_created_uploaded_published_listed_and_read_back_through_curl, src/github_tests.rs::the_token_rides_curls_stdin_and_never_its_argv_or_environment, src/github_tests.rs::a_write_without_the_token_is_refused_before_curl_starts, src/github_tests.rs::the_listing_is_read_whole_and_an_incomplete_page_is_refused, src/github_tests.rs::an_asset_redirect_off_the_cdn_is_refused_and_never_followed, src/github_tests.rs::a_hostile_curlrc_is_never_read, src/github_tests.rs::rows_are_jqs_tsv, src/github_tests.rs::a_launched_read_starts_each_curl_through_the_launch_and_none_once_interrupted, src/github_tests.rs::a_real_sigterm_reaches_a_post_cut_reads_curl_and_nothing_more_is_asked	curl child, loopback fake GitHub, thread-local fixture, signals and process groups	1,8

Every request is one /usr/bin/curl -q with the bearer on stdin as -H @-, the answer on stdout: the token and the assertion are never in an argument, the environment, a file of the client's scratch directory, or the log. A failure before releases.create is Failed::NotReleased naming the step and the version left unreleased (the live release untouched); 401/403, a refused key or none is Failed::Credential; a releases.create that went out and failed is Failed::Release (live state unknown), and one that never went out (curl refused or never started, or it never reached the endpoint: exit 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 35, 60) is Failed::NotReleased naming the finalized version. The token endpoint's answers are said by their status and error fields only, never their body. ### Proof dependency order

Easiest and highest-value first. Within a tier, specs are ordered by how few gaps block them, then by how many tests they retire. A pure core named in an effect spec (e.g. `turn_verdict` inside `rel-slice-drives-session`, `fence_liveness` inside `fence-reclaim-only-dead`, `durable_post_decision` inside `durable-post-refines-model`) is proved in its own earlier tier first, and the later spec composes it (gap 4).

## 3. Coverage

- 910 tests read. 880 map to one of the 372 specs; 30 are not spec samples (§4). Every cited test exists in the tree, and every test in the tree is cited or listed in §4 (checked mechanically against the `#[test]` fns of `build.rs`, `src/**` and `tests/**`).
- Spec samples per file: src/apple.rs 10, src/buildplan.rs 18, src/bundle.rs 3, src/changelog.rs 10, src/channel.rs 15, src/clean.rs 3, src/dev_app.rs 12, src/dev_identity.rs 12, src/dmg.rs 1, src/gates.rs 56, src/linux_release.rs 9, src/machines.rs 10, src/provision.rs 20, src/publish.rs 45, src/release_steps.rs 21, src/sign.rs 7, src/site/diff.rs 1, src/site/engine.rs 4, src/site/hosting/files.rs 5, src/site/hosting/key.rs 2, src/site/hosting/rest.rs 3, src/site/hosting/tests.rs 14, src/site/linux.rs 5, src/site/mod.rs 4, src/site/notes.rs 3, src/site/pages.rs 7, src/site/releases.rs 11, src/site/sitemap.rs 2, src/site/toolchain.rs 5, src/site/world.rs 2, src/snapshot.rs 1, src/transforms.rs 4, src/trust_facts.rs 28, src/trust_lane.rs 103, src/trust_oblig.rs 4, src/verify.rs 8, tests/it/apple_tier.rs 44, tests/it/build_once.rs 9, tests/it/changelog_golden.rs 11, tests/it/channel_floor_model.rs 6, tests/it/channel_latest.rs 24, tests/it/claim_landing_model.rs 3, tests/it/durable_post_intent_model.rs 9, tests/it/handoff_policy_bundle.rs 2, tests/it/hooks.rs 12, tests/it/journal_prefix_model.rs 1, tests/it/ledger_race.rs 19, tests/it/machine_roster.rs 19, tests/it/plist_stamp.rs 10, tests/it/publisher_fence_liveness.rs 17, tests/it/publisher_fence_model.rs 5, tests/it/release_fixtures.rs 6, tests/it/release_flow.rs 59, tests/it/release_lease.rs 9, tests/it/resume.rs 47, tests/it/signconf.rs 12, tests/it/site_sync.rs 26, tests/it/snapshot_take.rs 15, tests/it/trust_facts_model.rs 6.
- The biggest collapses (primary tests): `channel-head-safety` (12), `fence-reclaim-only-dead` (11), `rs-scan-release-page` (10), `plist-stamp-contract` (9).
- 369 specs retire at least one test as primary. 3 specs are only secondary for every test they cite (another, more specific spec retires each one) but each states a property of its own (a composed path or a second function), so it is still proved: `rel-x86-probe-record`, `rel-slice-drives-session`, `rl-lease-mutual-exclusion`.
- The release-flow invariants (`rf-*`) are stated over a `Backend` trait object and need gaps 2, 4, 8 and 11 at least; they cover the 59 spec-sample tests of `tests/it/release_flow.rs` but come last in practice.

## 4. Tests that are not spec samples

> Status 2026-10-16: deleted `src/changelog.rs::bounding_an_already_bounded_body_is_a_no_op`, `src/gates.rs::the_probe_source_still_carries_an_obligation` and `src/gates.rs::the_from_source_remedy_is_the_seed_first_recreator_then_a_seal`. `build.rs::rerun_and_dirty_inputs_are_one_source_of_truth` was not found (build.rs no longer exists; `SOURCE_INPUTS` moved to src/cutter.rs).
>
> Status 2026-10-17: `src/gates.rs::this_binary_carries_a_bounded_git_stamp` and `src/gates.rs::the_gate_knows_the_name_of_the_table_the_config_actually_carries` are deleted too, and the rows of these six are gone from the table below.
>
> No test reads a `.rs` file of this crate as text: a source property is held by construction, and its test drives the construction. The release POST body is `ReleaseBody`, made only by `release_body_for_post` (`src/publish.rs::an_oversized_notes_file_on_disk_is_bounded_at_the_post`); `after_upload_post` runs the settle before it asks the probe (`tests/it/durable_post_intent_model.rs::what_an_upload_does_after_its_post_is_one_table`); both withdrawals end through `withdraw_tag_then_unlock` (`tests/it/durable_post_intent_model.rs::recovery_cleanup_selector_refines_the_historical_recovery_model`); the release build's environment is `release_build_command`'s (`src/buildplan.rs::the_release_build_enables_no_development_seam`); an asset is read by its id alone, through the CDN redirect `github::open_asset` holds to the CDN origins (`src/github_tests.rs::an_asset_redirect_off_the_cdn_is_refused_and_never_followed`).

Actions: **delete** (samples nothing, or duplicates a spec sample); **move-to-bench** (a guard over
committed repository data, source text or the build environment: it belongs in an `aterm-census`
`verify census` guard row, a `pub doctor` check, or the bench, not in a function spec); **becomes-spec-after-fix**
(the code must change first, then a spec replaces the test).

| test | why | action |
|---|---|---|
| src/gates.rs::the_from_source_remedy_names_no_home_folder_and_is_atpkgs_command | Prose pinning plus equality of two remedy literals across crates. Define the recreator command once (gates.rs referencing `atpkg::toolchain::Toolchain::INSTALL_REMEDY`); then nothing is left to test. | delete |
| src/buildplan.rs::the_release_build_enables_no_development_seam | Repo-wide guard: walks every Cargo.toml for dev-seams, reads `[profile.release]` and `.cargo/config.toml`, and orders source text in `build_one`. Its argv half is `rel-cargo-build-args`. | move-to-bench |
| src/buildplan.rs::parser_reads_the_real_native_test_macho_section | Integration check that this build's linker embedded EXPECTED in `__aterm_upin`: a property of the build, not of the parser (`rel-thin-macho-pin-parser`). | move-to-bench |
| src/channel.rs::the_channel_is_exactly_the_one_clients_compiled_in | Binds the workspace Cargo.toml key to `aterm_update_core`'s build-script constants: repo state. | move-to-bench |
| src/gates.rs::the_checked_in_policy_passes_the_gate | Repo-state guard over handoff-policy.toml. | move-to-bench |
| src/linux_release.rs::the_worker_script_is_parsed_whole_before_it_runs | Pins a const shell script's shape and runs `bash -n` if bash exists (skips silently otherwise): an external-tool lint of a constant. | move-to-bench |
| src/site/notes.rs::the_changelog_page_is_the_reviewed_golden | Golden snapshot of rendered HTML; the renderer properties are `rel-site-render-markdown` and `rel-site-from-changelog`. | move-to-bench |
| src/site/notes.rs::the_release_bodies_page_is_the_reviewed_golden | Golden snapshot of `render_page`; its stable-tag sub-assertion is covered by `rel-site-stable` (cited there as a secondary sample). | move-to-bench |
| tests/it/export_policy.rs::the_export_selects_no_gitlink_and_none_of_the_withheld_files | Asserts what this repository's publish/manifest.txt selects: a guard over committed data. | move-to-bench |
| tests/it/export_policy.rs::the_public_clone_command_reads_as_one_line_and_admits_only_a_public_version | Checks publish/config.sh's `CHECK_CMD_DEFAULT` and runs grep on it: a tree-content guard over a shell file. | move-to-bench |
| tests/it/plist_stamp.rs::the_shipped_bundle_requires_native_execution | Template half is a data-file guard; stamping half follows from `plist-stamp-contract`. | move-to-bench |

No test is a pure regression of a removed bug with no general property: every regression test found
(the truncated-body transport fault, the v0.63.0 oversized image, the 0.92/0.93 journals, the
appcast 503) is an instance of one of the specs above.

## 5. Implied specs that look wrong, or disagree with the code

1. **Units disagree.** `src/changelog.rs::the_assembled_document_fits_what_github_accepts` and
   `src/publish.rs::an_oversized_notes_file_on_disk_is_bounded_at_the_post` assert BYTE length ≤ `L`.
   The bound is in UTF-16 units and the preamble has em dashes (3 bytes, 1 unit), so they pass only
   through slack at the section cut. The spec is `GL(doc) ≤ L`.
2. **Code bug.** `publish.rs::release_post_failure_detail` compares `posted_body_len` in bytes with the
   125 000-CHARACTER limit. An em-dash-heavy body within the UTF-16 limit but over 125 000 bytes is
   reported 'OVER IT'. It should use `github_length` (`rel-release-post-failure-detail` is stated with
   n as the caller passes it; fix the caller to pass `GL`).
3. **Boundary ambiguity.** `site/releases.rs::size_label` for n ∈ [999 500 000, 999 999 999] yields
   "1000 MB" (m < 1000, then rounded up) rather than "1.00 GB"; the switch is unsampled.
4. **Ordering by string.** `gates.rs::newest_published_source` orders `verified_at` as strings; RFC 3339
   timestamps with different UTC offsets misorder. The fixture uses one offset.
5. **Accepts more than it produces.** `bundle.rs::is_staging_dir_name` accepts names
   `staging_dir_name` never produces (leading zeros, digit strings over u64::MAX, e.g.
   `cut-007.noindex`), and `prune_staging_dirs` would delete them. Either tighten the predicate to
   `∃k. n = staging_dir_name(k)` or keep the spec as stated (§1 states the current code).
6. **"Exactly" overstated.** `dev_identity.rs::identity_hashes` matches `starts_with('"aterm dev"')`,
   so any suffix after the closing quote matches, and the returned hash can come from a
   `CSSMERR_TP_NOT_TRUSTED` (invalid) row. The test name says "exactly".
7. **Untested case-insensitivity.** `apple.rs::the_certificate_is_selected_by_digest_not_by_position`
   claims the digest compare ignores case, but both fixture digests are all digits.
8. **Vacuous on some hosts.** `src/linux_release.rs::the_worker_script_is_parsed_whole_before_it_runs`
   passes silently when `/bin/bash` is absent.
9. **Latency-looking bound.** `buildplan.rs::the_universal_cut_drives_a_session_on_its_x86_64_slice`
   uses a 300 ms bound in its 'silent' case. It is deterministic (the stand-in never opens the
   socket), but reads as a latency budget, against the one-minute hang-detector rule.
10. **Unreachable inputs.** `tests/it/resume.rs::first_incomplete_walks_the_step_order` feeds `done` lists
    with gaps or reordering that `Journal::save/load` make unreachable (`journal-roundtrip-valid`);
    the gap behaviour holds only for invalid journals.
11. **Even-count median.** `release.rs::estimates` takes `sorted[len/2]`, the upper median for an
    even count (a step with two or four runs on record), while its doc says the median.
    `rf-estimates-fmt-dur` states what the code does, and no test samples it.
12. **Goldens as definitions.** `tests/it/site_sync.rs::a_release_sync_rewrites_rebuilds_commits_pushes_deploys_and_verifies`
    and `release_bodies_render_releases_when_the_public_tree_has_no_changelog` compare /releases to
    golden HTML and pin commit-message prose; `ss-pages-rewrite` names the renderer as the
    definition instead.
13. **Partial resume coverage.** `tests/it/release_flow.rs::a_run_stopped_at_any_step_resumes_there_and_repeats_nothing_done`
    checks only calls the first run made before the stop step; `rf-resume-no-repeat` also needs the
    reserve/build rule, which other tests sample.
14. **Model-only negative controls.** The `tests/it/trust_facts_model.rs` negative controls
    (`the_pre_facts_receipt_reader_is_refused`, `the_unchecked_reuse_reader_is_refused`,
    `the_retired_floor_excuses_are_refused`) check that the MODEL refuses a hand-built state; they
    stay true whatever the code does (`tf-model-safety` is a model property, not a Rust contract).
    Only their trailing `assemble()`/`judge()` calls sample production (`judge-floor-rule`).
15. **Chunks disagreed; resolved from the code.** One chunk read `trust_facts::remeasured` as returning
    `None` across keys, another as `Err`. The code (`src/trust_facts.rs:3768`) returns
    `Result<Facts, String>`; `remeasure-fold-own-key` is stated with `Err`.
16. **Overlapping triage specs.** `rel-fresh-cut-triage` (one arm) is subsumed in substance by
    `rs-older-format-triage` (the whole table); prove the latter and retire both tests with it.
17. **Literal `#[test]` in strings.** `src/transforms.rs` carries a `"#[test]"` inside a string
    literal (a fixture), so a naive count of the crate's tests is one too many.

## 6. The three specs I would prove first (easiest, high value)

1. **`ledger-next-build`** (gap 3 only), with `exact-delete-absence` (seal only) as the warm-up.
   `next_build(tail, now) = max(now, tail + 1)` with a floor check is two u64 comparisons; it is an
   `omega`-class proof once E6 admits `max`. It is the arithmetic core of "a build number is never
   burned or reused" (`ledger-claim-cas-safety`, `rf-no-build-number-burned`, 11 tests between them)
   and retires its test outright. `rs-effective-min-build` and `rs-monotonic` (gaps 1, 3, scalar
   `Option<u64>`) follow with the same tactics.
2. **`rel-durable-post-decision`** (gaps 1, 3). Two `const fn`s from `bool`/`Option<bool>` to fieldless
   enums: an exhaustive case split. It is the pure decision under `durable-post-refines-model`
   (5 tests) — the "an issued intent never re-POSTs" safety rule — so proving it first lets the
   refinement bind to a certified function instead of an observed one. `lane-outcome-exit`,
   `rel-roster-duty`, `resume-attribution-agrees` and `successor-cut-options` (gap 1 only) are the
   same shape and nearly free alongside it.
3. **`rel-staging-dir-names`** (gaps 1, 3, 5, 7). `staging_dir_name` is injective and
   `is_staging_dir_name` recognises every name it produces, which guards what the pruner may
   delete (§5 item 5: it also accepts names `staging_dir_name` never produces).

Runner-up: the other pure string parsers blocked only by gaps 1/3/5/7 (`rs-claim-provenance`;
`rel-anon-probe-refusal`; `parse-prerelease-flag`). They wait on gap 1's `&str` carrier, which
nearly every spec in this crate needs (§1 histogram).
